# Experiment ledger: multiturn (Qwen3.5-397B-A17B-MXFP4 on 4x MI300A)

This ledger records every experiment and blocker from the multi-turn serving campaign, in the order they ran. Every experiment gets a row, including dead ends and rows that only produced a blocker and its fix, so nobody retries a refuted idea.

Columns:
- `tree`: the checkout SHA under test. `image`: the container or environment version.
- `hypothesis`: the mechanism tested. `prediction`: the expected number or outcome.
- `measured`: the median and spread, not one number. `gate`: pass or fail with the count.
- `decision`: accept, reject, or refuted, with one clause of reason.
- `scope`: the author's guess at where the finding holds (backend, gfx target, SKU, memory model, software version, or site). Site-scoped rows are never promoted.

exactness bit-exact | bm32-big is bit-exact (max_abs_diff=0.0 at every M) but net slower everywhere: 0.779x/0.767x/0.717x/0.698x/0.701x/0.946x/0.925x at M=16/64/192/337/512/919/1024 (stage1-only), combined stage1 plus stage2 (stage2 held at ttft-moe's own baseline, unchanged by this task) 0.772x/0.773x/0.962x/0.949x at M=337/512/919/1024, all below 1.0x, none within the 1.2 to 1.5x predicted range; measured redecode_mult_32 (1.178/1.283/1.533/1.596 at M=337/512/919/1024) came in higher than the predicted 1.12/1.19/1.36/1.40, so even the decode-reduction mechanism delivered less than predicted; amdhsa_metadata ground truth: production big and bm32-big both sit at 128 VGPRs/96 SGPRs (occupancy already capped at 4 waves/SIMD by VGPR/spill, per the ttft-moe row's own finding), bm32-big's LDS lands at exactly 65536 bytes as predicted but this is shown to be moot since production's own 32832-byte LDS already blocks a second concurrent workgroup by itself (2x32832 greater than 65536); VALUBusy 31.6/23.9 percent at M=337/1024 for bm32-big, below production's own 39.1/37.6 percent from the ttft-moe row, ruling out ALU saturation; the one allowed revision (atomic-add LDS reduction, 8192-byte buffer, 128 VGPR/31 SGPR/12 B scratch, matching or beating bm32-big on every static metric) measured uniformly worse (0.443x to 0.838x stage1-only across the same M range, worse than bm32-big at every point) and not bit-exact on big (max_abs_diff 256 to 2097152, all exact powers of 2, rel_l2 about 3.5e-8 to 4.3e-5, a regression from bm32-big's own full exactness); PMC (rocprofv3, kernel-include-regex moe_gemm_w4a16_stage1_kernel_bm32, M=337/1024) shows the atomic kernel issues 1.77x more LDS instructions than the plain kernel (1163408 vs 656512) and has lower VALUBusy (21.1/18.9 vs 31.1/23.8 percent), i.e. LDS-atomic contention (NWAVES=8 threads racing atomicAdd on the same address per cell) serializes rather than helping, the mechanism named for why the revision made things worse; bm32-small and bm32-atomic-small are both slower still (0.33 to 0.41x) and not exact (max_abs_diff 262144 to 2097152, small rel_l2, same signature the ttft-moe row already recorded for its own refuted small iteration), informational only since production never dispatches the 32-row path at these M | 13/13 not applicable, no server-boot correctness gate run: no candidate reached paired acceptance, all new code (moe_gemm_w4a16_stage1_bm32 and its small/atomic/atomic_small variants) is additive pybind-only, not called by fused_experts_mxfp4_hip's production dispatch, decode M=16/64 smoke-tested via the bench's own unchanged production-dispatch rows in the same process | refuted: this task's single-variable fix (single epilogue instead of doubled) is a real, directionally-correct improvement over the original ttft-moe attempt (0.70 to 0.95x here vs that attempt's 0.66 to 0.91x on the same big config at the same M points) but does not clear parity, let alone the 1.3x bar, at any M in range; Code read: prepare_chunk_indices and prepare_lens are wrapped in @tensor_cache (kernels/ops/attention/fla/utils.py:103-142), a 4-entry cache keyed by Python object identity; chunk.py's ChunkGatedDeltaRuleFunction.forward calls prepare_chunk_indices(cu_seqlens, CHUNK_SIZE) once per GDN layer, but cu_seqlens (=forward_metadata.query_start_loc, set once per forward, read not reassigned by all 45 layers) and CHUNK_SIZE=64 (a CPython int singleton) are the same objects on every call within one forward, so only the first GDN layer is a cache miss; layers 2-44 hit the cache and never sync.
